A cysteine knot (like in TGF-β, PDGF, NGF) has the following topology:
- One disulfide bond at the beginning (short loop)
- One disulfide bond that creates the &quot;knot&quot; by threading through the loop formed by other bonds
- The third disulfide goes through the knot

For the sequence: C41...C46...C56-W-C58-C59...C69...C82...C86

No, 46 &lt; 56 so 46-58 starts before 56-69 starts. And 58 &lt; 69, so 46-58 ends before 56-69 ends. These loops partially overlap.
- 59-86: Loop from 59 to 86. 59 is inside the 41-82 loop. 86 is outside the 41-82 loop. So this bond crosses the 41-82 loop.

For a cysteine knot, we&#x27;d want one bond to thread through a ring formed by another bond. The 59-86 bond crosses the 41-82 loop, which could create a knot-like topology.
